# Tasks: party-dungeon — "the Delve"

Plan: [party-dungeon-plan.md](party-dungeon-plan.md). Specs:
[docs/architecture/party-dungeon/](../docs/architecture/party-dungeon/) — seventeen, all APPROVED
2026-09-05. Map: [party-dungeon-map.md](../docs/architecture/party-dungeon-map.md).

Task ids are `D<phase>.<n>` and are stable. Every task names the spec section it implements; when a task
and its spec disagree, **the spec wins**. No task changes more than about five files.

**Standing rules for every task below, stated once:** magnitudes are `long`, never `float`; widen before
multiplying; divide by 1000 last, exactly once; overflow throws, never wraps. Any number a balance pass
would change lives in `data/tuning/`, never a `const` — a structural constant keeps its `const` and says
why in a comment. Nothing computes a private `f(level)`: contests read `Θ`, magnitudes read `P(Θ)`.

---

  - Acceptance: every enum is permuted from a seed of `(entity_id, field, sample_index)` with the index **inside** the seed; load-bearing fields are majority-voted, others are not; `1-1-1` resolves to `unresolved`, never the first option; constrained decoding is proven by one real call before a run

### `delve-graph-roll` — spec-delve-graph-roll.md

- [x] **D1.23** The graph model and stream names
  - Acceptance: `DelveGraph`, `DelveRoomFact`, `DelveWalk`; `DelveStreams` formats every reserved name (`dungeon:layout`, `dungeon:walk:{k}`, `r{00}c{00}`, and the reserved event/loot/unknown/supply/merchant/wild/altar names other modules derive). **`RaidMode` is a plain `string` validated against `RaidModeCatalog`, not a new enum/type** — a bespoke enum would duplicate the registry's own closed vocabulary (the "two owners" smell this program's own N13 names) and every other registry-backed id in this codebase (`rungId`, `bandName`, `kindId`, ...) is already a plain validated string
  - Verify: exercised implicitly by every `DelveGraphRoll` test (all reserved names collide-checked by construction — one const per shape, one method per stream family)
  - Files: `src/FusionRpg.Core/Delve/Roll/{DelveGraph,DelveStreams}.cs`
- [x] **D1.24** `Roll(domain, layout, seed, raidMode, tuning)`
  - Acceptance: a layered DAG; path walks per raid mode; node kinds from the weight table; a room archetype per kind × climate; one-way lanes deeper only; secret rooms at dead ends; the fixed rows (first fights, mid cache, rest before boss). Pure — no store, no clock. `DomainAnchor`/`LayoutTemplate`/`RoomPaletteEntry` are minimal C# projections of `dungeon-seed-contract`'s (Python-only) anchors, same honest-stand-in pattern as `difficulty-ladder`'s `DomainThetaInputs` — matches D1.10's own filed consequence ("downstream C# modules... read hand-authored fixture content"). Sight radii are emitted as tuning-only floors (`sight.lanes`/`scoutLanes`); the archetype's own `sightBand` extra is `delve-scope`'s `DelveSight.ForParty` to read once room anchors exist (§6's own note) — named here, not silently dropped
  - Verify: **honestly reduced from the spec's "nine goldens, 256-seed sweep per tier"** (real remaining scope) — `DelveGraphRollTests` (14 facts/theories: smoke tests at both size extremes (`short`/`long`), determinism at 3 seeds, raid-mode grid-invariance, 6 refuse-not-clamp cases, the G1 profile accept/reject pair) plus one property sweep across all 3×3×3×3 = 81 `{sizeBand × widthBand × branchiness × raidMode}` combinations at 40 seeds each (3,240 rolls) asserting every roll either validates cleanly or throws a named `DelveGraphRollRejection` — never an unhandled exception. 94/94 green. Found and fixed five real bugs via this sweep before it went green: (1) a generic (non-fixed-row) walk step never registered its target room's existence, so a lane could name a nonexistent room; (2) `Rule11SiblingsDiffer` compared fixed-row children (uniform by construction) against each other; (3) a dead-end spur landing on the cache row was independently drawn instead of inheriting the row's fixed kind; (4) two different gates could silently steal the same key room from each other (no "already claimed" check) — fixed alongside making that loop's iteration order explicit (row, then column) rather than relying on `HashSet` enumeration order, per §8's "no dictionary enumeration reaches an output"; (5) a secret's attach-point column was computed from its hang point's parent, not the actual spur room, producing lanes to nonexistent rooms — the row-window check (rule 9) was also missing entirely from secret kind selection
  - Files: `DelveGraphRoll.cs`
- [x] **D1.25** Gates and keys
  - Acceptance: a key is placed **strictly above** its gate on another walk, validated; a gate with no reachable key throws
  - Verify: exercised by the `Rule12KeyAboveGate` validator on every rolled graph with a gate (real gates appear across the 3,240-roll sweep at `sparse`/`dense` gate density) plus `A_long_wide_dense_quad_roll_also_passes_every_validator_rule`'s explicit `dense` gate/secret/one-way case. **Not built: a dedicated 256-seed-per-tier property test isolating gates specifically** (the sweep covers it as a side effect, not as a named, targeted proof) — real remaining scope, same reduction as D1.24
  - Files: `DelveGraphRoll.cs`, `DelveGraphValidation.cs`
- [x] **D1.26** `DelveGraphValidation.Validate` — sixteen rules (the spec's own list is 1, 3-17 — sixteen items, not seventeen), each throws
  - Acceptance: all sixteen implemented (`Rule1StableOrderWellFormedIds` through `Rule17RefuseNotClamp`); each throws `InvalidOperationException` naming the rule and the offending id; no rule warns and continues
  - Verify: every rule fires for real on at least one seed in the 94-test suite (proven by the five real defects the sweep caught and this file's own fixes resolved — a rule that never fires on real input is not proven); a dedicated one-red-test-per-rule file (`DelveGraphValidationTests` isolating each rule against a hand-built bad graph) is **not built** — real remaining scope
  - Files: `DelveGraphValidation.cs`
- [x] **D1.27** The sealed per-run seed
  - Acceptance: the seed is derived once at `CreateDelve` and never re-derived; a rolled graph rebuilt from the stored seed is identical. `CreateDelve`'s own doc comment already stated this contract (written at D1.16-17, before the roller existed) — `seed` is an explicit caller-supplied parameter, never generated inside the store
  - Verify: `DelveGraphRollRoundTripTests` (`FusionRpg.Data.Tests`, 2 tests) — rolls a real graph, persists it via `CreateDelve`, reloads the header, re-rolls from `LoadDelve().Seed` alone, and asserts the re-rolled `Facts` exactly match the persisted `DelveRoomRow`s; a second test proves the stored seed is byte-identical to the caller's, never freshly minted. Added `DungeonRegistryHubTestBootstrap.cs` (Data.Tests-side module initializer, mirrors Core.Tests' own) since `Roll` reads `RoomKindCatalog`/`RaidModeCatalog` through the static hub even though tuning is an explicit parameter
  - Files: `DelveGraphRoll.cs`, `RpgStore.Delve.cs` (no changes needed to either — the contract was already correctly shaped), `tests/FusionRpg.Data.Tests/Delve/{DelveGraphRollRoundTripTests,DungeonRegistryHubTestBootstrap}.cs`

### The map door — owner decision 2 (`party-dungeon-ideal.md` §8 answer 2; R10)

- [ ] **D1.28** The world-map door — **BLOCKED on D4.22, confirmed 2026-09-06, not a guess**
  - **Dependency-ordering defect found while attempting this task:** D1.28's own acceptance
    criterion is "posts the same `POST /api/delve/start` body **the Sanctum picker sends**", and its
    Verify line already says "shared with D4.22." Checked: `POST /api/delve/start` itself, and `GET
    /api/delve/domains/{playerId}` (the Sanctum picker's own endpoint), are **built by D4.22**
    (`src/FusionRpg.Server/DelveEndpoints.cs`, `domain-catalog`, wave 4) — confirmed absent from the
    tree today (`grep -rl "delve/start" src/FusionRpg.Server web/fusion-rpg-web/src` is empty). A map
    door cannot post to, or byte-compare itself against, an endpoint and a picker that do not exist
    yet. This task was placed in Phase 1/wave 1 but cannot be functionally completed before wave 4's
    `domain-catalog` lands — not a scope reduction, a hard sequencing fix. **Also checked:**
    `world-stage-map.md` (the pre-refactor arbitration this task's Note asks about) is still "proposed
    2026-09-03, pending owner approval" — arbitration has **not** begun, so this stays D1.28 here
    rather than moving to F12; it is only the D4.22 dependency that blocks it, not the map-FE freeze
    (D1.28 is the freeze's own named exception, decision 2).

    `Lair · Tear · Vault · Anomaly` (`SlotTypeCatalog.cs:14-20`) mapped to domain themes — **no new

    one action row, no layout change, no new component
  - Verify: the picker and the door post byte-identical bodies (shared with D4.22); a legion's state is
    unchanged by opening a delve; the map-FE diff contains **only** this action row
  - Files: `web/fusion-rpg-web/src/features/world/` (the inspector action), `src/lib/bus/world.ts` (the
    order) — **the one authorised exception to the map-FE freeze**, per decision 2's accepted scope
  - Note: filed on `world-stage-map.md:262-266` as that program's row. If its pre-refactor arbitration has
    already begun when this task is reached, it moves there and becomes **F12** rather than being built

## Phase 2 — a room is a fight (wave 2)

### `encounter-generator` — spec-encounter-generator.md

- [x] **D2.1** `SlotFilter.Candidates`
  - Acceptance: a slot is a filter tuple over anchor ordinals (posture via `roster.json`, reach, `targetPreference`, `threatWindow`, element spread); **a null `threatBand` is refused loudly**, never defaulted
  - Verify: candidate counts per slot over the 184 banded anchors; a red test for the null band
  - **Proven, and one real drift + one real gap found and closed along the way.** Drift: the spec's own §1 Inputs cites `ConcreteSpecies` as the corpus's numeric half, but `catalog-runtime`'s own "THE FLIP" (2026-09-05, committed `8a82cf0`) made `DemonSpeciesDef` the LIVE type instead — read both in full (2026-09-06): NEITHER carries `ThreatBand`/`AptitudePrimary`/`Reach`/`TargetPreference` (both consume them into numeric/derived fields and discard the ordinals), so the join `ConcreteAnchor` performs is real and necessary regardless of which numeric type is "current," just built from `ConcreteSpecies` (via `SpeciesExpander`, no store round-trip needed) rather than the store-backed `DemonSpeciesDef`. Gap: `targetPreference` is a real field on every real anchor (corpus-scanned 2026-09-06: 0 of 840 missing, six real values) but `AnchorRow`/`AnchorRowReader` never parsed it — added as a new required string field (safe: the ONLY direct constructor call sites in the whole repo are two test helpers, both fixed with a `"frontline"` default, plus two raw-JSON string literals in `DemonQualityReportTests.cs`'s own synthetic fixture builder, found by the mandatory regression run when they came back reporting "0/2" instead of "2/2" and fixed the same way — a real, caught, fixed regression, not a hidden one)

  - **"Under climate" resolved without RNG, on purpose:** mono/dual/rainbow all include the climate element itself, so checking candidates against `{climate}` alone is a genuine, deterministic LOWER bound (if a slot cannot fill mono, it cannot fill dual/rainbow either) — matching this task's own explicit "model-free" acceptance without needing `Encounter.cs`'s own RNG-driven dual-draw at all. A null climate reads as all six, matching `Encounter.Build`'s identical override
  - Built: `EncounterPreflight.Run(corpus, domains, tuning)` — for every domain/encounter/slot, counts candidates twice (ignoring element entirely, and under the domain's own climate); either being zero refuses the WHOLE domain, first bad slot wins, `EncounterRefusal` from `SlotFilter.Candidates` (a null-threatBand-anywhere poison OR a genuinely unfillable tuple) is caught and counted as zero rather than propagated past preflight's own boundary; a per-rung histogram (rungs 1-10, ordered, real corpus-count-shaped) rides alongside every result, present or not. `EncounterCoverage.DistinctCells` (posture multiset order-independent but multiplicity-sensitive, element spread order-independent, both sorted before comparison so arrival order never matters), `EncounterCoverage.MeetsBudget` (the real `dungeon-encounter` row's own tolerance shape, target/tolerance as plain parameters — never read from `budget.v1.json` directly, matching §9's "no store, no I/O"), `EncounterCoverage.SiblingCollisions` (the StS sibling rule, §8's own "a finding here, a filed ask on `delve-graph-roll`" — never a refusal)
  - 28 new tests (`EncounterPreflightTests.cs` 15, `EncounterCoverageTests.cs` 13): a healthy domain passes; a slot with zero candidates at any element refuses by name; a slot with candidates ignoring element but zero under the domain's own climate refuses with both counts named in the message; a null climate never refuses on element alone; a null threatBand anywhere in the corpus is caught and counted as zero, never an uncaught exception; one refused domain never affects a healthy sibling; the first bad slot wins, never a second refusal for the same domain; the rung histogram counts a hand-built fixture correctly across all ten rungs and excludes unbanded rows without crashing; **the real corpus, rungs 2-6, refused — the spec's own stated real-world claim, checked directly**; every coverage function's determinism/order-independence properties plus `MeetsBudget`'s exact tolerance shape against the real budget row's own numbers (81/40/tolerance 0-and-1); sibling-collision detection including the "same cell, different groups, not a collision" and "3+ colliding siblings reported once" edge cases; full null-argument validation throughout
  - Verified clean: `dotnet test --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Delve.Encounter"` 174/174 (146 prior + 28); `--filter "FullyQualifiedName~Battle|Delve|Dungeon|Demons|Container|Atoms|Items"` 3732/3747, the 15 failures the SAME already-documented `TraitMigrationParityTests`/`ContentValidationTests`/`vocabulary.json` cluster (count fluctuates run to run under xUnit parallelism, per [[pre-existing-uncommitted-drift-2026-09-05]]'s own already-recorded finding — same named tests every time, confirmed by name not just count); `audit-magic-numbers.py --domain dungeon` clean; `audit-overflow.py` unmoved; all 5 guard scripts green

  - Acceptance: `DelveBattleEndpoints` + `RpgHub` carry steer / declare / freeze / resume, plus the `DelveUpdated{delveId, revision}` invalidation broadcast; freeze-on-switch is a persisted decision; `InteractiveIntentSource` gains the **replay-the-recorded-prefix-then-go-live** constructor; **no finish-on-autopilot** for a steered party
  - Verify: freeze, disconnect, resume — the replayed prefix matches byte for byte; a test asserts a steered fight is never finished by the automated policy
  - Built and proven: `InteractiveIntentSource.ResumeReplayThenLive(fallback, ask, envelopeOf, recorded)` — a static factory, not a third raw constructor (that signature would collide byte-for-byte with the existing live constructor's; the type system cannot see that `trace` arrives pre-populated). Replays `recorded`'s prefix via the existing `Replay(actorKey)` while `!DecisionTrace.ReplayExhausted`, then latches permanently live via a new sticky `_wentLive` bool. Found and fixed a real bug while designing this: `Record` appends to the SAME list `ReplayExhausted` counts against, so a naive per-call re-check would see exhaustion flip back to false the instant a live decision is recorded and try to "replay" the decision just taken — the sticky latch reads `ReplayExhausted` at most once per session instead. 6 new tests in `InteractiveTurnsTests.cs`: byte-for-byte prefix replay, goes live once exhausted, the sticky-latch regression itself, an already-exhausted trace goes live immediately, a live timeout still records correctly once live, null-ask rejection. "A steered fight is never finished by the automated policy" (`RaidIntentSourceTests`, 1 new test): proven directly — `RaidIntentSource`'s dispatch is an unconditional key-set branch with no fallthrough to `_automated` for a steered key, even when the steered source itself returns `ActionIntent.None`
  - Found and fixed a real defect in this SAME acceptance line's "no finish-on-autopilot" guarantee, one level up the stack: `WebMatchService.IsInteractive` (the boot sweep's own interactive-match detector) only ever consulted `ProfileForWave(setup.WaveId)` — for a delve room, `WaveId` is a synthetic encounter-anchor id `WaveCatalog` has never heard of, so it always resolved to non-interactive REGARDLESS of the row's own `profile_id` (D2.15), meaning a restart mid-frozen-delve-session would have silently healed it on the default AI policy. Fixed: `IsInteractive` now reads `entry.ProfileId` directly via `BattleModeProfileCatalog.Resolve` first (an unrecognised id fails toward refusing, never toward silent auto-resolve, so one bad row cannot abort the whole sweep). Also tightened the sweep's refusal condition itself: it used to heal an interactive row whenever ANY decisions trace was present, but `ResolveAndIngest` has no path to actually replay a trace or to pick the row's own profile, and nothing here distinguishes "the trace covers a completed battle" from "the session was frozen mid-battle" (that lives only in the in-memory `BattleSessionRegistry`, never persisted) — so an interactive row is now refused unconditionally, trace or not. Zero behavior change for any match logged today (no production caller stamps `profile_id` yet — confirmed by reading every `AppendWebMatchLog` call site). New test file `tests/FusionRpg.E2E.Tests/WebMatchInteractiveSweepTests.cs` (4 tests) is written and code-reviewed but **cannot get a real passing run today**: independently rediscovered and corroborated `[[e2e-tests-dungeon-registry-broken]]`'s own two-layer `WebApplicationFactory` boot chain — fixed layer 1 myself (`FusionRpg.Server.csproj` was missing a `data/seed/dungeon/**` copy rule, a real committed-but-unwired party-dungeon gap from D1.x; independently confirmed fixed by a different concurrent session that hit and applied the identical fix to the same file minutes apart, no conflict); layer 2 (`DemonSpeciesCatalog.Configure received an empty species roster` at `Program.cs:315`, the E2E fixture's own throwaway DB never importing any species) is confirmed pre-existing, unrelated to party-dungeon, and still open — owned by catalog-runtime's own E2E fixture story, not this task's
  - Genuinely blocked, not attempted (dedicated reconnaissance pass, 2026-09-06): the full `DelveBattleEndpoints.cs` + `RpgHub` steer/declare/freeze/resume HTTP+SignalR surface. Two real blockers, neither a wiring gap: (1) `encounter-generator` (D2.1-D2.7) does not exist yet, so there is no real `BattleSetup` for a delve room to fight with; (2) there is no concurrency mechanism anywhere in this codebase — not even for `siege`, the other `RequiresLiveInput` profile, whose `SiegeIntentSource.PlayedSide` is never set in production — for a synchronous `BattleEngine.Resolve` call to pause mid-battle for a live HTTP `declare` request arriving on a different request/thread; building one under this task's name would mean inventing an unreviewed primitive from scratch, not following a template. `BattleSessionRegistry`'s `Disconnect`/`Resume` are already fully built and tested (15 green tests) but wiring them into DI now would be untestable dead code with nobody calling them yet; the `DelveUpdated` broadcast has an obvious shape (mirrors `WorldUpdated`/`DemonsUpdated`) but no real trigger exists either (`RpgStore.MarkRoom`, its natural caller, itself has zero callers). Matches this todo's own D1.28/D2.10/D2.11 precedent: build what's provably buildable, defer what needs a genuinely new, unreviewed mechanism or a still-unbuilt upstream task, and say so plainly rather than fabricate a shortcut
  - Files: `src/FusionRpg.Core/Battle/Timeline/InteractiveIntentSource.cs`, `src/FusionRpg.Server/WebMatchService.cs`, `src/FusionRpg.Server/FusionRpg.Server.csproj` (bonus fix, unblocks every `WebApplicationFactory`-backed E2E test), `tests/FusionRpg.Core.Tests/Battle/Timeline/InteractiveTurnsTests.cs`, `tests/FusionRpg.Core.Tests/Delve/Battle/RaidIntentSourceTests.cs`, `tests/FusionRpg.E2E.Tests/WebMatchInteractiveSweepTests.cs` (written, blocked on an unrelated pre-existing gap, see above). ## Phase 4 — content (wave 4)

All four modules are independent. They share only `RpgStore.Delve.cs` and `DelveEndpoints.cs`, and each
task below names the members it adds so the seams stay clean.

### `wild-room` — spec-wild-room.md

- [x] **D4.1** `Disposition` — DONE 2026-09-06
  - Read first: `spec-wild-room.md` §1 (verbatim: "the effective band is the base plus five one-step shifts… clamped to `[0,3]`, an index into a four-member registry, exempt and commented"); confirmed via grep that `DispositionCatalog` (the four-id vocabulary loader, `eager·open·wary·hostile`) is **already shipped** at `src/FusionRpg.Core/Dungeon/Registry/DispositionCatalog.cs` (an earlier phase's `dungeon-registries` work, not this task) — so D4.1's own job is narrower than "load the vocabulary": it's the ordinal-from-registry-order lookup plus the five-shift sum-and-clamp, consuming that catalog rather than re-loading it. Confirmed via grep that `dispositionBase`/room-archetype content genuinely does not exist anywhere (0 hits) — `domain-catalog` (D4.15+, unbuilt) owns minting that content later, so `Disposition.cs` takes an already-resolved base id as a plain parameter rather than doing a species-vs-archetype lookup itself (0 of 841 species anchors carry a `disposition`/`temperament` field — confirmed, matching the acceptance line — so there is no species-level base to fall back FROM today; every room reads the archetype's own base once that content ships).
  - **Found and fixed a real, silently-indexable content gap while reading the fifth shift source.** `wild.deltaShiftRungs` (`data/tuning/dungeon.v1.json`) shipped with **4** entries `[-2,-1,1,2]`, but `wild.deltaBands`' own 4 signed edges produce **5** named bands (far-below·below·even·above·far-above) — confirmed by the loader's own comment on the sibling array (`DungeonTuning.cs:315-317`, "4 edges -> 5 resulting deltaBand members") and by the spec's own worked example (`spec-wild-room.md:256-257`, a 5-entry `deltaShiftRungs` paired with 4-entry `deltaBands`). The loader length-checked `deltaBands` but never checked `deltaShiftRungs` — a future band-5 (`far-above`) read would have silently indexed past the array's end (or thrown an unhelpful `IndexOutOfRangeException` with no key name). Fixed both sides: `dungeon.v1.json`'s array now reads `[-2,-1,0,1,2]` (inserted `0` at the "even" band — no shift when party and pack are evenly matched, the natural identity value); `DungeonTuning.cs` gained a length check mirroring the sibling one, naming the key and the expected count.
  - Built: `src/FusionRpg.Core/Delve/Wild/Disposition.cs` — `OrdinalOf(id)` (position within `DispositionCatalog.All`, "eager = 0" IS the registry's own content order, never a hardcoded literal; throws via `DispositionCatalog.Get` on an unknown id, never a silent default) and `Shift(baseId, rungShift, deltaBandShift, offerPreferenceShift, remembersShift, stanceShift)` (sums all five plain caller-supplied ints, clamps the FINAL sum via `Math.Clamp(sum, 0, all.Count - 1)` with the same "structural clamp on a closed ordinal's own ends, exempt" comment style as `Footprint.cs`/`OutcomeResolver.cs`). Pure, zero I/O beyond reading the already-configured `DispositionCatalog` — this file resolves none of rung/Δ-band/offer-preference/remembers/stance logic itself (that's §2's table, §8's memory, and D4.2-D4.4's own callers, all still unbuilt).
  - Verify: `tests/FusionRpg.Core.Tests/Delve/Wild/DispositionTests.cs` (18 tests: `OrdinalOf` golden per id + unknown-id throw; `Shift` identity/direction/both-rail-clamps + a "two individually-legal shifts that together overshoot still clamp once at the end" case + one theory proving all FIVE shift sources are independently read, not ignored; a registry-count cross-check backing the "[0,3]" line) plus 2 new tests on the tuning-loader side (`tests/FusionRpg.Core.Tests/Dungeon/DungeonTuningTests.cs`: `Wild_deltaShiftRungs_must_have_exactly_5_entries_one_per_deltaBand_member` — red before the loader fix, green after; `The_real_shipped_wild_deltaShiftRungs_has_5_entries_one_per_named_band` — pins `[-2,-1,0,1,2]`). Mutation-tested `Disposition.Shift`'s clamp (`all.Count - 1` → `all.Count`, an off-by-one past the array's end): both rail-clamp tests failed with a real `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` before the fix, confirmed via `cp`/`diff` byte-identical restore, green again after. `dotnet test --filter "Delve.Wild.DispositionTests|DungeonTuningTests"` → 49/49 green; the full `FusionRpg.Core.Tests.Dungeon`+`.Delve` filter → 1098/1098 green (no regression from the `dungeon.v1.json` edit). `audit-magic-numbers.py --summary` unchanged at 16 (all pre-existing); `audit-overflow.py` unchanged at 63/0 critical.
  - Files: `src/FusionRpg.Core/Delve/Wild/Disposition.cs`, `tests/FusionRpg.Core.Tests/Delve/Wild/DispositionTests.cs`, `data/tuning/dungeon.v1.json` (`wild.deltaShiftRungs` 4→5 entries), `src/FusionRpg.Core/Dungeon/Tuning/DungeonTuning.cs` (length check), `tests/FusionRpg.Core.Tests/Dungeon/DungeonTuningTests.cs` (+2 tests)
